00049779 (mat4 m) {    float a00 = m[0][0], a01 = m[0][1], a02 = m[0][2], a03 = m[0][3];    float a10 = m[1][0], a11 = m[1][1], a12 = m[1][2], a13 = m[1][3];    float a20 = m[2][0], a21 = m[2][1], a22 = m[2][2], a23 = m[2][3];    float a30 = m[3][0], a31 = m[3][1], a32 = m[3][2], a33 = m[3][3];    float b00 = a00 * a11 - a01 * a10;    float b01 = a00 * a12 - a02 * a10;    float b02 = a00 * a13 - a03 * a10;    float b03 = a01 * a12 - a02 * a11;    float b04 = a01 * a13 - a03 * a11;    float b05 = a02 * a13 - a03 * a12;    float b06 = a20 * a31 - a21 * a30;    float b07 = a20 * a32 - a22 * a30;    float b08 = a20 * a33 - a23 * a30;    float b09 = a21 * a32 - a22 * a31;    float b10 = a21 * a33 - a23 * a31;    float b11 = a22 * a33 - a23 * a32;    float det = b00 * b11 - b01 * b10 + b02 * b09 + b03 * b08 -                 b04 * b07 + b05 * b06;    return mat4(        a11 * b11 - a12 * b10 + a13 * b09,        a02 * b10 - a01 * b11 - a03 * b09,        a31 * b05 - a32 * b04 + a33 * b03,        a22 * b04 - a21 * b05 - a23 * b03,        a12 * b08 - a10 * b11 - a13 * b07,        a00 * b11 - a02 * b08 + a03 * b07,        a32 * b02 - a30 * b05 - a33 * b01,        a20 * b05 - a22 * b02 + a23 * b01,        a10 * b10 - a11 * b08 + a13 * b06,        a01 * b08 - a00 * b10 - a03 * b06,        a30 * b04 - a31 * b02 + a33 * b00,        a21 * b02 - a20 * b04 - a23 * b00,        a11 * b07 - a10 * b09 - a12 * b06,        a00 * b09 - a01 * b07 + a02 * b06,        a31 * b01 - a30 * b03 - a32 * b00,        a20 * b03 - a21 * b01 + a22 * b00) / det;}
00049d7e (mat3 m) {    float a00 = m[0][0], a01 = m[0][1], a02 = m[0][2];    float a10 = m[1][0], a11 = m[1][1], a12 = m[1][2];    float a20 = m[2][0], a21 = m[2][1], a22 = m[2][2];    float b01 = a22 * a11 - a12 * a21;    float b11 = -a22 * a10 + a12 * a20;    float b21 = a21 * a10 - a11 * a20;    float det = a00 * b01 + a01 * b11 + a02 * b21;    return mat3(b01, (-a22 * a01 + a02 * a21), (a12 * a01 - a02 * a11),                b11, (a22 * a00 - a02 * a20), (-a12 * a00 + a02 * a10),                b21, (-a21 * a00 + a01 * a20), (a11 * a00 - a01 * a10)) / det;}

0004ad45                 bool is_non_triangular_conic_curve() {
0004ad7c                     // We consider a conic non-triangular as long as its weight isn't infinity.
0004addc                     // NOTE: "isinf == false" works on Mac Radeon GLSL; "!isinf" can get the wrong
0004ae3f                     // answer.

0004af04                 }

0004af90             float2 p0=p01.xy, p1=p01.zw, p2=p23.xy, p3=p23.zw;
0004afcf             if (is_conic_curve()) {
0004aff3                 // Conics are 3 points, with the weight in p3.
0004b032                 float w = p3.x;
0004b052                 p3 = p2;  // Duplicate the endpoint for shared code that also runs on cubics.
0004b0b0                 if (is_non_triangular_conic_curve()) {
0004b0e7                     // Convert the points to a trapeziodal hull that circumcscribes the conic.
0004b146                     float2 p1w = p1 * w;
0004b16f                     float T = .51;  // Bias outward a bit to ensure we cover the outermost samples.
0004b1d3                     float2 c1 = mix(p0, p1w, T);
0004b204                     float2 c2 = mix(p2, p1w, T);
0004b235                     float iw = 1 / mix(1, w, T);
0004b266                     p2 = c2 * iw;
0004b288                     p1 = c1 * iw;
0004b2aa                 }
0004b2bc             }
0004b2cb             // Translate the points to v0..3 where v0=0.
0004b304             float2 v1 = p1 - p0;
0004b325             float2 v2 = p2 - p0;
0004b346             float2 v3 = p3 - p0;
0004b368             // Reorder the points so v2 bisects v1 and v3.
0004b3a3             if (sign(cross_length_2d(v2, v1)) == sign(cross_length_2d(v2, v3))) {
0004b3f5                 float2 tmp = p2;
0004b416                 if (sign(cross_length_2d(v1, v2)) != sign(cross_length_2d(v1, v3))) {
0004b46c                     p2 = p1;  // swap(p2, p1)
0004b49a                     p1 = tmp;
0004b4b8                 } else {
0004b4d1                     p2 = p3;  // swap(p2, p3)
0004b4ff                     p3 = tmp;
0004b51d                 }
0004b52f             }
0004b53e             // Negative argsAttr.z means the join is an internal chop or circle, and both of
0004b59b             // those have empty joins. All we need is a bevel join.
0004b5df             if (argsAttr.z < 0) {
0004b601                 // +2 because we emit the beginning and ending edges twice (see above comment).
0004b661                 numEdgesInJoin = 1 + 2;
0004b689             }
0004b698             if (JOIN_TYPE >= 0 /*Is the join not a round type?*/) {
0004b6dc                 // Bevel and miter joins get 1 and 2 segments respectively.
0004b728                 // +2 because we emit the beginning and ending edges twice (see above comments).
0004b789                 numEdgesInJoin = sign(JOIN_TYPE) + 1 + 2;
0004b7c3             }
0004b7d2             layout(vertices = 1) out;
0004b7f9             in vec2 vsPt[];
0004b815             patch out mat4x2 rationalCubicXY;
0004b843             patch out float rationalCubicW;
0004b86f             patch out vec2 fanpoint;
0004b895             void main() {
0004b8af                 mat4x2 P = mat4x2(vsPt[0], vsPt[1], vsPt[2], vsPt[3]);
0004b8f6                 float numSegments;
0004b919                 if (isinf(P[3].y)) {
0004b93e                     // This is a conic.
0004b966                     float w = P[3].x;
0004b98c                     numSegments = wangs_formula_conic(PRECISION, P[0], P[1], P[2], w);
0004b9e3                     // Convert to a rational cubic in projected form.
0004ba29                     rationalCubicXY = mat4x2(P[0],
0004ba5c                                              mix(vec4(P[0], P[2]), (P[1] * w).xyxy, 2.0/3.0),
0004baba                                              P[2]);
0004baee                     rationalCubicW = fma(w, 2.0/3.0, 1.0/3.0);
0004bb2d                 } else {
0004bb46                     // This is a cubic.
0004bb6e                     numSegments = wangs_formula_cubic(PRECISION, P[0], P[1], P[2], P[3], mat2(1));
0004bbd1                     rationalCubicXY = P;
0004bbfa                     rationalCubicW = 1;
0004bc22                 }
0004bc34                 fanpoint = vsPt[4];
0004bc59                 // Tessellate the first side of the patch into numSegments triangles.
0004bcaf                 gl_TessLevelOuter[0] = min(numSegments, MAX_TESSELLATION_SEGMENTS);
0004bd04                 // Leave the other two sides of the patch as single segments.
0004bd52                 gl_TessLevelOuter[1] = 1.0;
0004bd7e                 gl_TessLevelOuter[2] = 1.0;
0004bdab                 // Changing the inner level to 1 when numSegments == 1 collapses the entire
0004be07                 // patch to a single triangle. Otherwise, we need an inner level of 2 so our curve
0004be6a                 // triangles have an interior point to originate from.
0004beb1                 gl_TessLevelInner[0] = min(numSegments, 2.0);
0004beef             }
0004befe             layout(vertices = 1) out;
0004bf25             in vec2 P[];
0004bf3e             patch out mat4x2 rationalCubicXY;
0004bf6c             patch out float rationalCubicW;
0004bf99             void main() {
0004bfb3                 float w = -1;  // w<0 means a cubic.
0004bfe8                 vec2 p1w = P[1];
0004c009                 if (isinf(P[3].y)) {
0004c02e                     // This patch is actually a conic. Project to homogeneous space.
0004c083                     w = P[3].x;
0004c0a3                     p1w *= w;
0004c0c1                 }
0004c0d4                 // Chop the curve at T=1/2.
0004c100                 vec2 ab = (P[0] + p1w) * .5;
0004c12d                 vec2 bc = (p1w + P[2]) * .5;
0004c15a                 vec2 cd = (P[2] + P[3]) * .5;
0004c188                 vec2 abc = (ab + bc) * .5;
0004c1b3                 vec2 bcd = (bc + cd) * .5;
0004c1de                 vec2 abcd = (abc + bcd) * .5;
0004c20d                 float n0, n1;
0004c22b                 if (w < 0 || isinf(w)) {
0004c254                     if (w < 0) {
0004c275                         // The patch is a cubic. Calculate how many segments are required to
0004c2d2                         // linearize each half of the curve.
0004c30f                         n0 = wangs_formula_cubic(PRECISION, P[0], ab, abc, abcd, mat2(1));
0004c36a                         n1 = wangs_formula_cubic(PRECISION, abcd, bcd, cd, P[3], mat2(1));
0004c3c5                         rationalCubicW = 1;
0004c3f1                     } else {
0004c40e                         // The patch is a triangle (a conic with infinite weight).
0004c461                         n0 = n1 = 1;
0004c486                         rationalCubicW = -1;  // In the next stage, rationalCubicW<0 means triangle.
0004c4eb                     }
0004c501                     rationalCubicXY = mat4x2(P[0], P[1], P[2], P[3]);
0004c547                 } else {
0004c560                     // The patch is a conic. Unproject p0..5. w1 == w2 == w3 when chopping at .5.
0004c5c2                     // (See SkConic::chopAt().)
0004c5f2                     float r = 2.0 / (1.0 + w);
0004c621                     ab *= r, bc *= r, abc *= r;
0004c651                     // Put in "standard form" where w0 == w2 == w4 == 1.
0004c69a                     float w_ = inversesqrt(r);  // Both halves have the same w' when chopping at .5.
0004c6ff                     // Calculate how many segments are needed to linearize each half of the curve.
0004c762                     n0 = wangs_formula_conic(PRECISION, P[0], ab, abc, w_);
0004c7ae                     n1 = wangs_formula_conic(PRECISION, abc, bc, P[2], w_);
0004c7fa                     // Covert the conic to a rational cubic in projected form.
0004c849                     rationalCubicXY = mat4x2(P[0],
0004c87c                                              mix(float4(P[0],P[2]), p1w.xyxy, 2.0/3.0),
0004c8d4                                              P[2]);
0004c908                     rationalCubicW = fma(w, 2.0/3.0, 1.0/3.0);
0004c947                 }
0004c95a                 gl_TessLevelOuter[0] = min(n1, MAX_TESSELLATION_SEGMENTS);
0004c9a5                 gl_TessLevelOuter[1] = 1.0;
0004c9d1                 gl_TessLevelOuter[2] = min(n0, MAX_TESSELLATION_SEGMENTS);
0004ca1d                 // Changing the inner level to 1 when n0 == n1 == 1 collapses the entire patch to a
0004ca81                 // single triangle. Otherwise, we need an inner level of 2 so our curve triangles
0004cae3                 // have an interior point to originate from.
0004cb20                 gl_TessLevelInner[0] = min(max(n0, n1), 2.0);
0004cb5e             }
0004cb6d             layout(triangles, equal_spacing, ccw) in;
0004cba4             uniform vec4 sk_RTAdjust;
0004cbcb             patch in mat4x2 rationalCubicXY;
0004cbf8             patch in float rationalCubicW;
0004cc24             void main() {
0004cc3e                 vec2 vertexpos;
0004cc5e                 if (rationalCubicW < 0) {  // rationalCubicW < 0 means a triangle now.
0004ccb5                     vertexpos = (gl_TessCoord.x != 0) ? rationalCubicXY[0]
0004cd00                               : (gl_TessCoord.y != 0) ? rationalCubicXY[1]
0004cd4b                                                       : rationalCubicXY[2];
0004cd97                 } else {
0004cdb0                     // Locate our parametric point of interest. T ramps from [0..1/2] on the left
0004ce12                     // edge of the triangle, and [1/2..1] on the right. If we are the patch's
0004ce70                     // interior vertex, then we want T=1/2. Since the barycentric coords are
0004cecd                     // (1/3, 1/3, 1/3) at the interior vertex, the below fma() works in all 3
0004cf2b                     // scenarios.
0004cf4d                     float T = fma(.5, gl_TessCoord.y, gl_TessCoord.z);
0004cf95                     mat4x3 P = mat4x3(rationalCubicXY[0], 1,
0004cfd2                                       rationalCubicXY[1], rationalCubicW,
0004d01c                                       rationalCubicXY[2], rationalCubicW,
0004d066                                       rationalCubicXY[3], 1);
0004d0a4                     vertexpos = eval_rational_cubic(P, T);
0004d0df                     if (all(notEqual(gl_TessCoord.xz, vec2(0)))) {
0004d122                         // We are the interior point of the patch; center it inside
0004d176                         // [C(0), C(.5), C(1)].
0004d1a6                         vertexpos = (P[0].xy + vertexpos + P[3].xy) / 3.0;
0004d1f1                     }
0004d207                 }
0004d21a                 gl_Position = vec4(vertexpos * sk_RTAdjust.xz + sk_RTAdjust.yw, 0.0, 1.0);
0004d275             }
0004d284             layout(triangles, equal_spacing, ccw) in;
0004d2bb             uniform vec4 sk_RTAdjust;
0004d2e2             patch in mat4x2 rationalCubicXY;
0004d30f             patch in float rationalCubicW;
0004d33a             patch in vec2 fanpoint;
0004d35f             void main() {
0004d379                 // Locate our parametric point of interest. It is equal to the barycentric
0004d3d4                 // y-coordinate if we are a vertex on the tessellated edge of the triangle patch,
0004d436                 // 0.5 if we are the patch's interior vertex, or N/A if we are the fan point.
0004d494                 // NOTE: We are on the tessellated edge when the barycentric x-coordinate == 0.
0004d4f4                 float T = (gl_TessCoord.x == 0.0) ? gl_TessCoord.y : 0.5;
0004d53f                 mat4x3 P = mat4x3(rationalCubicXY[0], 1,
0004d578                                   rationalCubicXY[1], rationalCubicW,
0004d5be                                   rationalCubicXY[2], rationalCubicW,
0004d604                                   rationalCubicXY[3], 1);
0004d63e                 vec2 vertexpos = eval_rational_cubic(P, T);
0004d67b                 if (gl_TessCoord.x == 1.0) {
0004d6a8                     // We are the anchor point that fans from the center of the curve's contour.
0004d709                     vertexpos = fanpoint;
0004d733                 } else if (gl_TessCoord.x != 0.0) {
0004d767                     // We are the interior point of the patch; center it inside [C(0), C(.5), C(1)].
0004d7cc                     vertexpos = (P[0].xy + vertexpos + P[3].xy) / 3.0;
0004d813                 }
0004d826                 gl_Position = vec4(vertexpos * sk_RTAdjust.xz + sk_RTAdjust.yw, 0.0, 1.0);
0004d881             }
0004d893         }
0004d89e         float edgeID = float(sk_VertexID >> 1);
0004d8ce         if ((sk_VertexID & 1) != 0) {
0004d8f4             edgeID = -edgeID;
0004d912         }
0004d91d         // Edge #2 extends to the miter point.
0004d94c         if (abs(edgeID) == 2 && %s) {
0004d972             strokeOutset *= miter_extent(cosTheta, JOIN_TYPE/*miterLimit*/);
0004d9bf         }
0004d9ca     void main() {
0004d9dc         // Our patch is composed of exactly "numTotalCombinedSegments + 1" stroke-width edges that
0004da3f         // run orthogonal to the curve and make a strip of "numTotalCombinedSegments" quads.
0004da9c         // Determine which discrete edge belongs to this invocation. An edge can either come from a
0004db00         // parametric segment or a radial one.
0004db2f         float numSegmentsInJoin = tcsJoinArgs0.x;
0004db61         float numTotalCombinedSegments = numSegmentsInJoin + tcsTessArgs[0].x + tcsTessArgs[1].x +
0004dbc4                                          tcsTessArgs[2].x;
0004dbff         float combinedEdgeID = round(gl_TessCoord.x * numTotalCombinedSegments);
0004dc50         float strokeOutset = gl_TessCoord.y * 2.0 - 1.0;
0004dc8a         // Furthermore, the vertex shader may have chopped the curve into 3 different sections.
0004dcea         // Determine which section we belong to, and where we fall relative to its first edge.
0004dd49         float2 p0, p1, p2, p3;
0004dd68         vec2 tan0;
0004dd7b         float numParametricSegments, radsPerSegment;
0004ddb0         if (combinedEdgeID < numSegmentsInJoin || numSegmentsInJoin == numTotalCombinedSegments) {
0004de13             // Our edge belongs to the join preceding the curve.
0004de54             p3 = p2 = p1 = p0 = tcsPts01[0].xy;
0004de84             tan0 = tcsJoinArgs0.zw;
0004dea8             numParametricSegments = 1;
0004decf             radsPerSegment = tcsJoinArgs1.x;
0004defc             strokeOutset = clamp(strokeOutset, tcsJoinArgs1.y, tcsJoinArgs1.z);
0004df4c             strokeOutset *= (combinedEdgeID == 1.0) ? tcsJoinArgs0.y : 1.0;
0004df98         } else if ((combinedEdgeID -= numSegmentsInJoin) < tcsTessArgs[0].x) {
0004dfe7             // Our edge belongs to the first curve section.
0004e023             p0=tcsPts01[0].xy, p1=tcsPts01[0].zw, p2=tcsPt2Tan0[0].xy, p3=tcsPts01[1].xy;
0004e07d             tan0 = tcsPt2Tan0[0].zw;
0004e0a2             numParametricSegments = tcsTessArgs[0].y;
0004e0d8             radsPerSegment = tcsTessArgs[0].z;
0004e107         } else if ((combinedEdgeID -= tcsTessArgs[0].x) < tcsTessArgs[1].x) {
0004e155             // Our edge belongs to the second curve section.
0004e192             p0=tcsPts01[1].xy, p1=tcsPts01[1].zw, p2=tcsPt2Tan0[1].xy, p3=tcsPts01[2].xy;
0004e1ec             tan0 = tcsPt2Tan0[1].zw;
0004e211             numParametricSegments = tcsTessArgs[1].y;
0004e247             radsPerSegment = tcsTessArgs[1].z;
0004e276         } else {
0004e287             // Our edge belongs to the third curve section.
0004e2c3             combinedEdgeID -= tcsTessArgs[1].x;
0004e2f3             p0=tcsPts01[2].xy, p1=tcsPts01[2].zw, p2=tcsPt2Tan0[2].xy, p3=tcsEndPtEndTan.xy;
0004e350             tan0 = tcsPt2Tan0[2].zw;
0004e375             numParametricSegments = tcsTessArgs[2].y;
0004e3ab             radsPerSegment = tcsTessArgs[2].z;
0004e3da         }
0004e3e4         float2 tan1 = tcsEndPtEndTan.zw;
0004e40d         bool isFinalEdge = (gl_TessCoord.x == 1);
0004e43f         float w = -1.0;  // w<0 means the curve is an integral cubic.
0004e485         if (isinf(p3.y)) {
0004e4a0             w = p3.x;  // The curve is actually a conic.
0004e4d9             p3 = p2;  // Setting p3 equal to p2 works for the remaining rotational logic.
0004e533         }
0004e53e         // Unpack the curve args from the vertex shader.
0004e577         mat4x2 P;
0004e589         mat2 tangents;
0004e5a0         if (gl_InvocationID == 0) {
0004e5c4             // This is the first section of the curve.
0004e5fb             P = mat4x2(vsPts01[0], vsPts23[0]);
0004e62b             tangents = mat2(vsTans01[0]);
0004e655         } else if (gl_InvocationID == 1) {
0004e680             // This is the middle section of the curve.
0004e6b8             P = mat4x2(vsPts23[0].zw, vsPts45[0], vsPts67[0].xy);
0004e6fa             tangents = mat2(vsTans01[0].zw, vsTans23[0].xy);
0004e737         } else {
0004e748             // This is the final section of the curve.
0004e77f             P = mat4x2(vsPts67[0], vsPts89[0]);
0004e7af             tangents = mat2(vsTans23[0]);
0004e7d9         }
0004e7e4         // Calculate the number of parametric segments. The final tessellated strip will be a
0004e842         // composition of these parametric segments as well as radial segments.
0004e892         float w = isinf(P[3].y) ? P[3].x : -1.0; // w<0 means the curve is an integral cubic.
0004e8f0         float numParametricSegments;
0004e915         if (w < 0.0) {
0004e92c             numParametricSegments = wangs_formula_cubic(MAX_SCALE * PRECISION, P[0], P[1], P[2],
0004e98d                                                         P[3], mat2(1));
0004e9d5         } else {
0004e9e6             numParametricSegments = wangs_formula_conic(MAX_SCALE * PRECISION, P[0], P[1], P[2], w);
0004ea4b         }
0004ea55         if (P[0] == P[1] && P[2] == P[3]) {
0004ea81             // This is how the patch builder articulates lineTos but Wang's formula returns
0004eadd             // >>1 segment in this scenario. Assign 1 parametric segment.
0004eb27             numParametricSegments = 1.0;
0004eb50         }
0004eb5b         // Determine the curve's total rotation. The vertex shader ensures our curve does not rotate
0004ebc0         // more than 180 degrees or inflect, so the inverse cosine has enough range.
0004ec15         float cosTheta = cosine_between_vectors(tangents[0], tangents[1]);
0004ec60         float rotation = acos(cosTheta);
0004ec8a         // Adjust sign of rotation to match the direction the curve turns.
0004ecd5         // NOTE: Since the curve is not allowed to inflect, we can just check F'(.5) x F''(.5).
0004ed35         // NOTE: F'(.5) x F''(.5) has the same sign as (P2 - P0) x (P3 - P1)
0004ed82         float turn = isinf(P[3].y) ? cross2d(P[1] - P[0], P[2] - P[1])
0004edc9                                    : cross2d(P[2] - P[0], P[3] - P[1]);
0004ee11         if (turn == 0.0) {  // This is the case for joins and cusps where points are co-located.
0004ee72             turn = determinant(tangents);
0004ee9c         }
0004eea6         if (turn < 0.0) {
0004eec0             rotation = -rotation;
0004eee2         }
0004eeed         // Calculate the number of evenly spaced radial segments to chop this section of the curve
0004ef50         // into. Radial segments divide the curve's rotation into even steps. The final tessellated
0004efb4         // strip will be a composition of both parametric and radial segments.
0004f003         float numRadialSegments = abs(rotation) * NUM_RADIAL_SEGMENTS_PER_RADIAN;
0004f055         numRadialSegments = max(ceil(numRadialSegments), 1.0);
0004f095         // The first and last edges are shared by both the parametric and radial sets of edges, so
0004f0f8         // the total number of edges is:
0004f121         //
0004f12c         //   numCombinedEdges = numParametricEdges + numRadialEdges - 2
0004f174         //
0004f17f         // It's also important to differentiate between the number of edges and segments in a strip:
0004f1e4         //
0004f1ef         //   numCombinedSegments = numCombinedEdges - 1
0004f227         //
0004f232         // So the total number of segments in the combined strip is:
0004f277         //
0004f282         //   numCombinedSegments = numParametricEdges + numRadialEdges - 2 - 1
0004f2d1         //                       = numParametricSegments + 1 + numRadialSegments + 1 - 2 - 1
0004f32e         //                       = numParametricSegments + numRadialSegments - 1
0004f37f         //
0004f38a         float numCombinedSegments = numParametricSegments + numRadialSegments - 1.0;
0004f3e0         if (P[0] == P[3] && tangents[0] == tangents[1]) {
0004f41a             // The vertex shader intentionally disabled our section. Set numCombinedSegments to 0.
0004f47d             numCombinedSegments = 0.0;
0004f4a4         }
0004f4af         // Pack the args for the evaluation stage.
0004f4e2         tcsPts01[gl_InvocationID] = vec4(P[0], P[1]);
0004f518         tcsPt2Tan0[gl_InvocationID] = vec4(P[2], tangents[0]);
0004f557         tcsTessArgs[gl_InvocationID] = vec3(numCombinedSegments, numParametricSegments,
0004f5af                                             rotation / numRadialSegments);
0004f5fa         if (gl_InvocationID == 2) {
0004f61e             tcsEndPtEndTan = vec4(P[3], tangents[1]);
0004f654         }
0004f65f         barrier();
0004f673         // Tessellate a quad strip with enough segments for the join plus all 3 curve sections
0004f6d2         // combined.
0004f6e7         float numTotalCombinedSegments = tcsJoinArgs0.x + tcsTessArgs[0].x + tcsTessArgs[1].x +
0004f747                                          tcsTessArgs[2].x;
0004f783         if (tcsJoinArgs0.x != 0.0 && tcsJoinArgs0.x != numTotalCombinedSegments) {
0004f7d6             // We are tessellating a quad strip with both a single-sided join and a double-sided
0004f837             // stroke. Add one more edge to the join. This new edge will fall parallel with the
0004f897             // first edge of the stroke, eliminating artifacts on the transition from single
0004f8f4             // sided to double.
0004f914             ++tcsJoinArgs0.x;
0004f932             ++numTotalCombinedSegments;
0004f95a         }
0004f965         numTotalCombinedSegments = min(numTotalCombinedSegments, MAX_TESSELLATION_SEGMENTS);
0004f9c2         gl_TessLevelInner[0] = numTotalCombinedSegments;
0004f9fb         gl_TessLevelInner[1] = 2.0;
0004fa1f         gl_TessLevelOuter[0] = 2.0;
0004fa43         gl_TessLevelOuter[1] = numTotalCombinedSegments;
0004fa7c         gl_TessLevelOuter[2] = 2.0;
0004faa0         gl_TessLevelOuter[3] = numTotalCombinedSegments;
0004fad9     }
0004fae0     float2 tangent, strokeCoord;
0004fb01     if (combinedEdgeID != 0 && !isFinalEdge) {
0004fb30         // Compute the location and tangent direction of the stroke edge with the integral id
0004fb8e         // "combinedEdgeID", where combinedEdgeID is the sorted-order index of parametric and radial
0004fbf3         // edges. Start by finding the tangent function's power basis coefficients. These define a
0004fc56         // tangent direction (scaled by some uniform value) as:
0004fc96         //                                                 |T^2|
0004fcd7         //     Tangent_Direction(T) = dx,dy = |A  2B  C| * |T  |
0004fd18         //                                    |.   .  .|   |1  |
0004fd59         float2 A, B, C = p1 - p0;
0004fd7b         float2 D = p3 - p0;
0004fd97         if (w >= 0.0) {
0004fdaf             // P0..P2 represent a conic and P3==P2. The derivative of a conic has a cumbersome
0004fe0e             // order-4 denominator. However, this isn't necessary if we are only interested in a
0004fe6f             // vector in the same *direction* as a given tangent line. Since the denominator scales
0004fed3             // dx and dy uniformly, we can throw it out completely after evaluating the derivative
0004ff36             // with the standard quotient rule. This leaves us with a simpler quadratic function
0004ff97             // that we use to find a tangent.
0004ffc5             C *= w;
0004ffd9             B = .5*D - C;
0004fff3             A = (w - 1.0) * D;
00050012             p1 *= w;
00050027         } else {
00050038             float2 E = p2 - p1;
00050058             B = E - C;
0005006f             A = fma(float2(-3), E, D);
00050096         }
000500a0         // FIXME(crbug.com/800804,skbug.com/11268): Consider normalizing the exponents in A,B,C at
00050103         // this point in order to prevent fp32 overflow.
0005013d         // Now find the coefficients that give a tangent direction from a parametric edge ID:
0005019b         //
000501a6         //                                                                 |parametricEdgeID^2|
00050206         //     Tangent_Direction(parametricEdgeID) = dx,dy = |A  B_  C_| * |parametricEdgeID  |
00050266         //                                                   |.   .   .|   |1                 |
000502c6         //
000502d1         float2 B_ = B * (numParametricSegments * 2.0);
00050308         float2 C_ = C * (numParametricSegments * numParametricSegments);
00050352         // Run a binary search to determine the highest parametric edge that is located on or before
000503b7         // the combinedEdgeID. A combined ID is determined by the sum of complete parametric and
00050418         // radial segments behind it. i.e., find the highest parametric edge where:
0005046c         //
00050477         //    parametricEdgeID + floor(numRadialSegmentsAtParametricT) <= combinedEdgeID
000504d0         //
000504db         float lastParametricEdgeID = 0.0;
00050505         float maxParametricEdgeID = min(numParametricSegments - 1.0, combinedEdgeID);
0005055b         // FIXME(crbug.com/800804,skbug.com/11268): This normalize() can overflow.
000505ae         float2 tan0norm = normalize(tan0);
000505d9         float negAbsRadsPerSegment = -abs(radsPerSegment);
00050614         float maxRotation0 = (1.0 + combinedEdgeID) * abs(radsPerSegment);
0005065f         for (int exp = %i - 1; exp >= 0; --exp) {
00050691             // Test the parametric edge at lastParametricEdgeID + 2^exp.
000506da             float testParametricID = lastParametricEdgeID + exp2(float(exp));
00050728             if (testParametricID <= maxParametricEdgeID) {
00050763                 float2 testTan = fma(float2(testParametricID), A, B_);
000507aa                 testTan = fma(float2(testParametricID), testTan, C_);
000507f0                 float cosRotation = dot(normalize(testTan), tan0norm);
00050837                 float maxRotation = fma(testParametricID, negAbsRadsPerSegment, maxRotation0);
00050896                 maxRotation = min(maxRotation, PI);
000508ca                 // Is rotation <= maxRotation? (i.e., is the number of complete radial segments
0005092a                 // behind testT, + testParametricID <= combinedEdgeID?)
00050972                 if (cosRotation >= cos(maxRotation)) {
000509a9                     // testParametricID is on or before the combinedEdgeID. Keep it!
000509fe                     lastParametricEdgeID = testParametricID;
00050a3b                 }
00050a4d             }
00050a5b         }
00050a66         // Find the T value of the parametric edge at lastParametricEdgeID.
00050ab2         float parametricT = lastParametricEdgeID / numParametricSegments;
00050afd         // Now that we've identified the highest parametric edge on or before the
00050b4f         // combinedEdgeID, the highest radial edge is easy:
00050b8b         float lastRadialEdgeID = combinedEdgeID - lastParametricEdgeID;
00050bd4         // Find the angle of tan0, or the angle between tan0norm and the positive x axis.
00050c2e         float angle0 = acos(clamp(tan0norm.x, -1.0, 1.0));
00050c69         angle0 = tan0norm.y >= 0.0 ? angle0 : -angle0;
00050ca1         // Find the tangent vector on the edge at lastRadialEdgeID.
00050ce5         float radialAngle = fma(lastRadialEdgeID, radsPerSegment, angle0);
00050d30         tangent = float2(cos(radialAngle), sin(radialAngle));
00050d6e         float2 norm = float2(-tangent.y, tangent.x);
00050da4         // Find the T value where the tangent is orthogonal to norm. This is a quadratic:
00050dfe         //
00050e09         //     dot(norm, Tangent_Direction(T)) == 0
00050e3d         //
00050e48         //                         |T^2|
00050e71         //     norm * |A  2B  C| * |T  | == 0
00050e9f         //            |.   .  .|   |1  |
00050ec8         //
00050ed3         float a=dot(norm,A), b_over_2=dot(norm,B), c=dot(norm,C);
00050f15         float discr_over_4 = max(b_over_2*b_over_2 - a*c, 0.0);
00050f55         float q = sqrt(discr_over_4);
00050f7b         if (b_over_2 > 0.0) {
00050f99             q = -q;
00050fad         }
00050fb7         q -= b_over_2;
00050fcf         // Roots are q/a and c/q. Since each curve section does not inflect or rotate more than 180
00051033         // degrees, there can only be one tangent orthogonal to "norm" inside 0..1. Pick the root
00051095         // nearest .5.
000510ac         float _5qa = -.5*q*a;
000510ca         float2 root = (abs(fma(q,q,_5qa)) < abs(fma(a,c,_5qa))) ? float2(q,a) : float2(c,q);
00051127         float radialT = (root.t != 0.0) ? root.s / root.t : 0.0;
00051168         radialT = clamp(radialT, 0.0, 1.0);
00051195         if (lastRadialEdgeID == 0.0) {
000511bc             // The root finder above can become unstable when lastRadialEdgeID == 0 (e.g., if
0005121a             // there are roots at exatly 0 and 1 both). radialT should always == 0 in this case.
0005127b             radialT = 0.0;
00051296         }
000512a1         // Now that we've identified the T values of the last parametric and radial edges, our final
00051306         // T value for combinedEdgeID is whichever is larger.
00051344         float T = max(parametricT, radialT);
00051372         // Evaluate the cubic at T. Use De Casteljau's for its accuracy and stability.
000513c9         float2 ab = unchecked_mix(p0, p1, T);
000513f7         float2 bc = unchecked_mix(p1, p2, T);
00051425         float2 cd = unchecked_mix(p2, p3, T);
00051453         float2 abc = unchecked_mix(ab, bc, T);
00051482         float2 bcd = unchecked_mix(bc, cd, T);
000514b1         float2 abcd = unchecked_mix(abc, bcd, T);
000514e4         // Evaluate the conic weight at T.
0005150f         float u = unchecked_mix(1.0, w, T);
0005153b         float v = w + 1 - u;  // == mix(w, 1, T)
0005156c         float uv = unchecked_mix(u, v, T);
00051598         // If we went with T=parametricT, then update the tangent. Otherwise leave it at the radial
000515fc         // tangent found previously. (In the event that parametricT == radialT, we keep the radial
0005165f         // tangent.)
00051674         if (T != radialT) {
00051690             tangent = (w >= 0.0) ? bc*u - ab*v : bcd - abc;
000516cc         }
000516d7         strokeCoord = (w >= 0.0) ? abc/uv : abcd;
00051709     } else {
00051716         // Edges at the beginning and end of the strip use exact endpoints and tangents. This
00051774         // ensures crack-free seaming between instances.
000517ad         tangent = (combinedEdgeID == 0) ? tan0 : tan1;
000517e4         strokeCoord = (combinedEdgeID == 0) ? p0 : p3;
0005181b     }
00051822     // Find how many parametric segments this stroke requires.
00051861     float numParametricSegments;
00051882     if (w < 0) {
00051893         if (p0 == p1 && p2 == p3) {
000518b7             numParametricSegments = 1; // a line
000518e8         } else {
000518f9             numParametricSegments = wangs_formula_cubic(PRECISION, p0, p1, p2, p3, AFFINE_MATRIX);
0005195c         }
00051966     } else {
00051973         numParametricSegments = wangs_formula_conic(PRECISION,
000519b2                                                     AFFINE_MATRIX * p0,
000519fa                                                     AFFINE_MATRIX * p1,
00051a42                                                     AFFINE_MATRIX * p2, w);
00051a8e     }
00051a95     // Returns the length squared of the largest forward difference from Wang's cubic formula.
00051af4     float wangs_formula_max_fdiff_pow2(float2 p0, float2 p1, float2 p2, float2 p3,
00051b47                                        float2x2 matrix) {
00051b81         float2 d0 = matrix * (fma(float2(-2), p1, p2) + p0);
00051bbe         float2 d1 = matrix * (fma(float2(-2), p2, p3) + p1);
00051bfb         return max(dot(d0,d0), dot(d1,d1));
00051c27     }
00051c2d     float wangs_formula_cubic(float _precision_, float2 p0, float2 p1, float2 p2, float2 p3,
00051c8a                               float2x2 matrix) {
00051cbb         float m = wangs_formula_max_fdiff_pow2(p0, p1, p2, p3, matrix);
00051d03         return max(ceil(sqrt(%f * _precision_ * sqrt(m))), 1.0);
00051d44     }
00051d4a     float wangs_formula_cubic_log2(float _precision_, float2 p0, float2 p1, float2 p2, float2 p3,
00051dac                                    float2x2 matrix) {
00051de2         float m = wangs_formula_max_fdiff_pow2(p0, p1, p2, p3, matrix);
00051e2a         return ceil(log2(max(%f * _precision_ * _precision_ * m, 1.0)) * .25);
00051e79     }
00051e80     float wangs_formula_conic_pow2(float _precision_, float2 p0, float2 p1, float2 p2, float w) {
00051ee2         // Translate the bounding box center to the origin.
00051f1e         float2 C = (min(min(p0, p1), p2) + max(max(p0, p1), p2)) * 0.5;
00051f66         p0 -= C;
00051f77         p1 -= C;
00051f88         p2 -= C;
00051f9a         // Compute max length.
00051fb9         float m = sqrt(max(max(dot(p0,p0), dot(p1,p1)), dot(p2,p2)));
00052000         // Compute forward differences.
00052028         float2 dp = fma(float2(-2.0 * w), p1, p0) + p2;
00052060         float dw = abs(fma(-2.0, w, 2.0));
0005208c         // Compute numerator and denominator for parametric step size of linearization. Here, the
000520ee         // epsilon referenced from the cited paper is 1/precision.
00052131         float rp_minus_1 = max(0.0, fma(m, _precision_, -1.0));
00052171         float numer = length(dp) * _precision_ + rp_minus_1 * dw;
000521b3         float denom = 4 * min(w, 1.0);
000521db         return numer/denom;
000521f7     }
000521fd     float wangs_formula_conic(float _precision_, float2 p0, float2 p1, float2 p2, float w) {
0005225a         float n2 = wangs_formula_conic_pow2(_precision_, p0, p1, p2, w);
000522a3         return max(ceil(sqrt(n2)), 1.0);
000522cc     }
000522d2     float wangs_formula_conic_log2(float _precision_, float2 p0, float2 p1, float2 p2, float w) {
00052334         float n2 = wangs_formula_conic_pow2(_precision_, p0, p1, p2, w);
0005237d         return ceil(log2(max(n2, 1.0)) * .5);
000523ab     }
000523b2     // Find the starting and ending tangents.
000523e0     float2 tan0 = ((p0 == p1) ? (p1 == p2) ? p3 : p2 : p1) - p0;
00052421     float2 tan1 = p3 - ((p3 == p2) ? (p2 == p1) ? p0 : p1 : p2);
00052462     if (tan0 == float2(0)) {
0005247f         // The stroke is a point. This special case tells us to draw a stroke-width circle as a
000524df         // 180 degree point stroke instead.
0005250b         tan0 = float2(1,0);
00052527         tan1 = float2(-1,0);
00052544     }
0005254b     float cross2d(vec2 a, vec2 b) {
0005256f         return determinant(mat2(a,b));
00052596     }
0005259d     // Find which direction the curve turns.
000525ca     // NOTE: Since the curve is not allowed to inflect, we can just check F'(.5) x F''(.5).
00052626     // NOTE: F'(.5) x F''(.5) has the same sign as (P2 - P0) x (P3 - P1)
0005266f     float turn = cross_length_2d(p2 - p0, p3 - p1);
000526a3     float combinedEdgeID = abs(edgeID) - numEdgesInJoin;
000526dc     if (combinedEdgeID < 0) {
000526fa         tan1 = tan0;
0005270f         // Don't let tan0 become zero. The code as-is isn't built to handle that case. tan0=0
0005276d         // means the join is disabled, and to disable it with the existing code we can leave
000527ca         // tan0 equal to tan1.
000527e9         if (lastControlPoint != p0) {
0005280f             tan0 = p0 - lastControlPoint;
00052839         }
00052843         turn = cross_length_2d(tan0, tan1);
0005286f     }
00052876     // Calculate the curve's starting angle and rotation.
000528b0     float cosTheta = cosine_between_vectors(tan0, tan1);
000528e9     float rotation = acos(cosTheta);
0005290e     if (turn < 0) {
00052922         // Adjust sign of rotation to match the direction the curve turns.
0005296d         rotation = -rotation;
0005298b     }
00052992     float numRadialSegments;
000529af     float strokeOutset = sign(edgeID);
000529d6     if (combinedEdgeID < 0) {
000529f4         // We belong to the preceding join. The first and final edges get duplicated, so we only
00052a55         // have "numEdgesInJoin - 2" segments.
00052a84         numRadialSegments = numEdgesInJoin - 2;
00052ab4         numParametricSegments = 1;  // Joins don't have parametric segments.
00052b01         p3 = p2 = p1 = p0;  // Colocate all points on the junction point.
00052b4b         // Shift combinedEdgeID to the range [-1, numRadialSegments]. This duplicates the first
00052bab         // edge and lands one edge at the very end of the join. (The duplicated final edge will
00052c0b         // actually come from the section of our strip that belongs to the stroke.)
00052c5f         combinedEdgeID += numRadialSegments + 1;
00052c90         // We normally restrict the join on one side of the junction, but if the tangents are
00052cee         // nearly equivalent this could theoretically result in bad seaming and/or cracks on the
00052d4f         // side we don't put it on. If the tangents are nearly equivalent then we leave the join
00052db0         // double-sided.
00052dc9         float sinEpsilon = 1e-2;  // ~= sin(180deg / 3000)
00052e04         bool tangentsNearlyParallel =
00052e2a                 (abs(turn) * inversesqrt(dot(tan0, tan0) * dot(tan1, tan1))) < sinEpsilon;
00052e85         if (!tangentsNearlyParallel || dot(tan0, tan1) < 0) {
00052ec3             // There are two edges colocated at the beginning. Leave the first one double sided
00052f23             // for seaming with the previous stroke. (The double sided edge at the end will
00052f7f             // actually come from the section of our strip that belongs to the stroke.)
00052fd7             if (combinedEdgeID >= 0) {
00052ffe                 strokeOutset = (turn < 0) ? min(strokeOutset, 0) : max(strokeOutset, 0);
00053057             }
00053065         }
0005306f         combinedEdgeID = max(combinedEdgeID, 0);
000530a0     } else {
000530ad         // We belong to the stroke.
000530d1         float maxCombinedSegments = NUM_TOTAL_EDGES - numEdgesInJoin - 1;
0005311b         numRadialSegments = max(ceil(abs(rotation) * NUM_RADIAL_SEGMENTS_PER_RADIAN), 1);
00053175         numRadialSegments = min(numRadialSegments, maxCombinedSegments);
000531be         numParametricSegments = min(numParametricSegments,
000531f9                                     maxCombinedSegments - numRadialSegments + 1);
0005324b     }
00053252     // Additional parameters for emitTessellationCode().
0005328b     float radsPerSegment = rotation / numRadialSegments;
000532c4     float numCombinedSegments = numParametricSegments + numRadialSegments - 1;
00053313     bool isFinalEdge = (combinedEdgeID >= numCombinedSegments);
00053353     if (combinedEdgeID > numCombinedSegments) {
00053383         strokeOutset = 0;  // The strip has more edges than we need. Drop this one.
000533d7     }
000533de     float2 p0=pts01Attr.xy, p1=pts01Attr.zw, p2=pts23Attr.xy, p3=pts23Attr.zw;
0005342d     float2 lastControlPoint = argsAttr.xy;
00053458     float w = -1;  // w<0 means the curve is an integral cubic.
00053498     if (is_conic_curve()) {
000534b4         // Conics are 3 points, with the weight in p3.
000534eb         w = p3.x;
000534fd         p3 = p2;  // Setting p3 equal to p2 works for the remaining rotational logic.
00053553     }

000539c0     float ab_cosTheta = dot(a,b);

00066188         // the curve's points to ensure it gets disabled by the tessellation stages.
000661dd         P[1] = P[2] = P[3] = P[0];
00066200         // Since the points are colocated, this curve will register as a line, which overrides
0006625f         // innerTangents as [tan0, tan0]. Setting tan1=tan0 as well results in all tangents and all
000662c3         // points being equal, which disables every section of the curve.
0006630d         tan1 = tan0;
00066322     }
00066329     // Calculate the number of segments to chop the join into.
00066368     float cosTheta = cosine_between_vectors(prevJoinTangent, tan0);
000663ac     float joinRotation = (cosTheta == 1) ? 0 : acos(cosTheta);
000663eb     if (cross_length_2d(prevJoinTangent, tan0) < 0) {
00066421         joinRotation = -joinRotation;
00066447     }
0006644d     float joinRadialSegments = abs(joinRotation) * NUM_RADIAL_SEGMENTS_PER_RADIAN;
000664a0     float numSegmentsInJoin = (joinRadialSegments != 0 /*Is the join non-empty?*/ &&
000664f5                                JOIN_TYPE >= 0 /*Is the join not a round type?*/)
00066546             ? sign(JOIN_TYPE) + 1  // Non-empty bevel joins have 1 segment and miters have 2.
000665a4             : ceil(joinRadialSegments);  // Otherwise round up the number of radial segments.
00066603     // Extends the middle join edge to the miter point.
0006663b     float innerJoinRadiusMultiplier = 1;
00066664     if (JOIN_TYPE > 0 /*Is the join a miter type?*/) {
0006669b         innerJoinRadiusMultiplier = miter_extent(cosTheta, JOIN_TYPE/*miterLimit*/);
000666f0     }
000666f7     // Clamps join geometry to the exterior side of the junction.
00066739     float2 joinOutsetClamp = float2(-1, 1);
00066765     if (joinRadialSegments > .1 /*Does the join rotate more than 1/10 of a segment?*/) {
000667be         // Only clamp if the join angle is large enough to guarantee there won't be cracks on
0006681c         // the interior side of the junction.
0006684a         joinOutsetClamp = (joinRotation < 0) ? float2(-1, 0) : float2(0, 1);
00066897     }
0006689e     // Pack join args for the tessellation control stage.
000668d8     vsJoinArgs0 = float4(numSegmentsInJoin, innerJoinRadiusMultiplier, prevJoinTangent);
00066931     vsJoinArgs1 = float3(joinRotation / numSegmentsInJoin, joinOutsetClamp);
0006697f     // Now find where to chop the curve so the resulting sub-curves are convex and do not rotate
000669e0     // more than 180 degrees. We don't need to worry about cusps because the caller chops those out
00066a44     // on the CPU. Start by finding the cubic's power basis coefficients. These define the bezier
00066aa6     // curve as:
00066ab7     //
00066abe     //                                    |T^3|
00066aee     //     Cubic(T) = x,y = |A  3B  3C| * |T^2| + P0
00066b23     //                      |.   .   .|   |T  |
00066b53     //
00066b5a     // And the tangent direction (scaled by a uniform 1/3) will be:
00066b9e     //
00066ba5     //                                                 |T^2|
00066be2     //     Tangent_Direction(T) = dx,dy = |A  2B  C| * |T  |
00066c1f     //                                    |.   .  .|   |1  |
00066c5c     //
00066c63     float2 C = P[1] - P[0];
00066c7f     float2 D = P[2] - P[1];
00066c9b     float2 E = P[3] - P[0];
00066cb7     float2 B = D - C;
00066ccd     float2 A = fma(float2(-3), D, E);
00066cf4     // Now find the cubic's inflection function. There are inflections where F' x F'' == 0.
00066d50     // We formulate this as a quadratic equation:  F' x F'' == aT^2 + bT + c == 0.
00066da3     // See: https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2005/01/p1000-loop.pdf
00066e02     // NOTE: We only need the roots, so a uniform scale factor does not affect the solution.
00066e5f     float a = cross_length_2d(A, B);
00066e84     float b = cross_length_2d(A, C);
00066ea9     float c = cross_length_2d(B, C);
00066ece     float b_over_2 = b*.5;
00066ee9     float discr_over_4 = b_over_2*b_over_2 - a*c;
00066f1c     float2x2 innerTangents = float2x2(0);
00066f46     if (discr_over_4 <= 0) {
00066f63         // The curve does not inflect. This means it might rotate more than 180 degrees instead.
00066fc4         // Craft a quadratic whose roots are the points were rotation == 180 deg and 0. (These are
00067027         // the points where the tangent is parallel to tan0.)
00067065         //
00067070         //      Tangent_Direction(T) x tan0 == 0
000670a1         //      (AT^2 x tan0) + (2BT x tan0) + (C x tan0) == 0
000670e0         //      (A x C)T^2 + (2B x C)T + (C x C) == 0  [[because tan0 == P1 - P0 == C]]
00067138         //      bT^2 + 2cT + 0 == 0  [[because A x C == b, B x C == c]]
00067180         //
0006718b         // NOTE: When P0 == P1 then C != tan0, C == 0 and these roots will be undefined. But that's
000671ef         // ok because when P0 == P1 the curve cannot rotate more than 180 degrees anyway.
00067249         a = b;
00067258         b_over_2 = c;
0006726e         c = 0;
0006727d         discr_over_4 = b_over_2*b_over_2;
000672a7         innerTangents[0] = -C;
000672c6     }
000672cd     // Solve our quadratic equation for the chop points. This is inspired by the quadratic formula
00067330     // from Numerical Recipes in C.
00067354     float q = sqrt(discr_over_4);
00067376     if (b_over_2 > 0) {
0006738e         q = -q;
0006739e     }
000673a4     q -= b_over_2;
000673b7     float2 chopT = float2((a != 0) ? q/a : 0,
000673e5                           (q != 0) ? c/q : 0);
00067415     // Reposition any chop points that fall outside ~0..1 and clear their innerTangent.
0006746d     int numOutside = 0;
00067485     if (chopT[0] <= kParametricEpsilon || chopT[0] >= 1 - kParametricEpsilon) {
000674d5         innerTangents[0] = float2(0);
000674fb         ++numOutside;
00067511     }
00067517     if (chopT[1] <= kParametricEpsilon || chopT[1] >= 1 - kParametricEpsilon) {
00067567         // Swap places with chopT[0]. This ensures chopT[0] is outside when numOutside > 0.
000675c3         chopT = chopT.ts;
000675dd         innerTangents = float2x2(0,0, innerTangents[0]);
00067616         ++numOutside;
0006762c     }
00067632     if (numOutside == 2) {
0006764d         chopT[1] = 2/3.0;
00067667     }
0006766d     if (numOutside >= 1) {
00067688         chopT[0] = (chopT[1] <= .5) ? chopT[1] * .5 : fma(chopT[1], .5, .5);
000676d5     }
000676dc     // Sort the chop points.
000676f9     if (chopT[0] > chopT[1]) {
00067718         chopT = chopT.ts;
00067732         innerTangents = float2x2(innerTangents[1], innerTangents[0]);
00067778     }
0006777f     // If the curve is a straight line, point, or conic, don't chop it into sections after all.
000677df     if ((P[0] == P[1] && P[2] == P[3]) || isinf(P[3].y)) {
0006781a         chopT = float2(0);
00067835         innerTangents = float2x2(tan0, tan0);
00067863     }
0006786a     // Chop the curve at chopT[0] and chopT[1].
0006789a     float4 ab = unchecked_mix(P[0].xyxy, P[1].xyxy, chopT.sstt);
000678db     float4 bc = unchecked_mix(P[1].xyxy, P[2].xyxy, chopT.sstt);
0006791c     float4 cd = isinf(P[3].y) ? P[2].xyxy : unchecked_mix(P[2].xyxy, P[3].xyxy, chopT.sstt);
00067979     float4 abc = unchecked_mix(ab, bc, chopT.sstt);
000679ad     float4 bcd = unchecked_mix(bc, cd, chopT.sstt);
000679e1     float4 abcd = unchecked_mix(abc, bcd, chopT.sstt);
00067a18     float4 middle = unchecked_mix(abc, bcd, chopT.ttss);
00067a52     // Find tangents at the chop points if an inner tangent wasn't specified.
00067aa0     if (innerTangents[0] == float2(0)) {
00067ac9         innerTangents[0] = bcd.xy - abc.xy;
00067af5     }
00067afb     if (innerTangents[1] == float2(0)) {
00067b24         innerTangents[1] = bcd.zw - abc.zw;
00067b50     }
00067b57     // Pack curve args for the tessellation control stage.
00067b92     vsPts01 = float4(P[0], ab.xy);
00067bb5     vsPts23 = float4(abc.xy, abcd.xy);
00067bdc     vsPts45 = middle;
00067bf2     vsPts67 = float4(abcd.zw, bcd.zw);
00067c19     vsPts89 = float4(cd.zw, P[3]);
00067c3c     vsTans01 = float4(tan0, innerTangents[0]);
00067c6b     vsTans23 = float4(innerTangents[1], tan1);

00067cbd half distance = 7.96875*(texColor.r - 0.50196078431);

0006dd04                 uniform int isRadiusIncreasing;
0006dd34                 uniform int isFocalOnCircle;
0006dd61                 uniform int isWellBehaved;
0006dd8c                 uniform int isSwapped;

0006de4b                 half4 main(float2 p) {
0006de72                     float t = -1;
0006de94                     half v = 1; // validation flag, set to negative to discard fragment later
0006def3                     float x_t = -1;
0006df17                     if (bool(isFocalOnCircle)) {
0006df48                         x_t = dot(p, p) / p.x;
0006df77                     } else if (bool(isWellBehaved)) {
0006dfad                         x_t = length(p) - p.x * invR1;
0006dfe4                     } else {
0006e001                         float temp = p.x * p.x - p.y * p.y;
0006e03e                         // Only do sqrt if temp >= 0; this is significantly slower than checking
0006e09f                         // temp >= 0 in the if statement that checks r(t) >= 0. But GPU may break if
0006e104                         // we sqrt a negative float. (Although I havevn't observed that on any
0006e163                         // devices so far, and the old approach also does sqrt negative value
0006e1c1                         // without a check.) If the performance is really critical, maybe we should
0006e225                         // just compute the area where temp and x_t are always valid and drop all
0006e287                         // these ifs.
0006e2ad                         if (temp >= 0) {
0006e2d6                             if (bool(isSwapped) || !bool(isRadiusIncreasing)) {
0006e326                                 x_t = -sqrt(temp) - p.x * invR1;
0006e367                             } else {
0006e38c                                 x_t = sqrt(temp) - p.x * invR1;
0006e3cc                             }
0006e3ea                         }
0006e404                     }
0006e41b                     // The final calculation of t from x_t has lots of static optimizations but only
0006e480                     // do them when x_t is positive (which can be assumed true if isWellBehaved is
0006e4e3                     // true)
0006e500                     if (!bool(isWellBehaved)) {
0006e530                         // This will still calculate t even though it will be ignored later in the
0006e593                         // pipeline to avoid a branch

0006e617                         }
0006e631                     }
0006e647                     if (bool(isRadiusIncreasing)) {
0006e67b                         if (bool(isNativelyFocal)) {
0006e6b0                             t = x_t;
0006e6d5                         } else {
0006e6f6                             t = x_t + fx;
0006e720                         }
0006e73a                     } else {
0006e757                         if (bool(isNativelyFocal)) {
0006e78c                             t = -x_t;
0006e7b2                         } else {
0006e7d3                             t = -x_t + fx;
0006e7fe                         }
0006e818                     }
0006e82f                     if (bool(isSwapped)) {
0006e85a                         t = 1 - t;
0006e87d                     }
0006e894                     return half4(half(t), v, 0, 0);
0006e8c8                 }
0006e8da             
0006e8e8                     dashAlpha += half(%s(x - currDash.x, d) * %s(currDash.y - x, d));
0006e93e                     dashAlpha += half(%s(x - nextDash.x, d) * %s(nextDash.y - x, d));
0006e994                     dashAlpha += half(%s(x - prevDash.x, d) * %s(prevDash.y - x, d));
0006e9ea                     dashAlpha = min(dashAlpha, 1);
0006ea1d                     edgeAlpha *= dashAlpha;
0006ea49             
0006ea57                 int2 coords = int2(%s.x, %s.y);
0006ea87                 int texIdx = coords.x >> 13;
0006eab4                 float2 unormTexCoords = float2(coords.x & 0x1FFF, coords.y);
0006eb01             
0006eb0f                 float texIdx = 0;
0006eb31                 float2 unormTexCoords = float2(%s.x, %s.y);
0006eb6d             
0006eb7b                 float2 coord = float2(%s.x, %s.y);
0006ebae                 float texIdx = floor(coord.x * exp2(-13));
0006ebe9                 float2 unormTexCoords = float2(coord.x - texIdx * exp2(13), coord.y);
0006ec3f             
0006ec4d                     // The two boundary dash intervals are stored in wrapDashes.xy and .zw and fed
0006ecb0                     // to the fragment shader as a varying.
0006ecec                     float4 wrapDashes;
0006ed13                     half lastIntervalLength = mod(6.28318530718, half(dashParams.y));
0006ed69                     // We can happen to be perfectly divisible.
0006eda9                     if (0 == lastIntervalLength) {
0006eddc                         lastIntervalLength = half(dashParams.y);
0006ee1d                     }
0006ee33                     // Let 'l' be the last interval before reaching 2 pi.
0006ee7d                     // Based on the phase determine whether (l-1)th, l-th, or (l+1)th interval's
0006eede                     // "corresponding" dash appears in the l-th interval and is closest to the 0-th
0006ef42                     // interval.
0006ef63                     half offset = 0;
0006ef88                     if (-dashParams.w >= lastIntervalLength) {
0006efc7                          offset = half(-dashParams.y);
0006effe                     } else if (dashParams.w > dashParams.y - lastIntervalLength) {
0006f051                          offset = half(dashParams.y);
0006f087                     }
0006f09d                     wrapDashes.x = -lastIntervalLength + offset - dashParams.w;
0006f0ed                     // The end of this dash may be beyond the 2 pi and therefore clipped. Hence the
0006f151                     // min.
0006f16d                     wrapDashes.y = min(wrapDashes.x + dashParams.x, 0);
0006f1b6                     // Based on the phase determine whether the -1st, 0th, or 1st interval's
0006f213                     // "corresponding" dash appears in the 0th interval and is closest to l.
0006f270                     offset = 0;
0006f290                     if (dashParams.w >= dashParams.x) {
0006f2c8                         offset = half(dashParams.y);
0006f2fd                     } else if (-dashParams.w > dashParams.y - dashParams.x) {
0006f34b                         offset = half(-dashParams.y);
0006f381                     }
0006f397                     wrapDashes.z = lastIntervalLength + offset - dashParams.w;
0006f3e6                     wrapDashes.w = wrapDashes.z + dashParams.x;
0006f426                     // The start of the dash we're considering may be clipped by the start of the
0006f488                     // circle.
0006f4a7                     wrapDashes.z = max(wrapDashes.z, lastIntervalLength);
0006f4f1             
0006f4ff                     float linearDist;
0006f525                     angleToEdge = clamp(angleToEdge, -3.1415, 3.1415);
0006f56c                     linearDist = diameter * sin(angleToEdge / 2);
0006f5ae                     return saturate(linearDist + 0.5);
0006f5e5             
0006f5f3                 int texIdx = 0;
0006f613                 float2 unormTexCoords = float2(%s.x, %s.y);
0006f64f            
0006f65c         half4 main(float2 coord) {
0006f67f             half t = half(coord.x);
0006f6a3             float4 s, b;
0006f6bc             // thresholds1_7.w is mid point for intervals (0,7) and (8,15)
0006f707             if (%d <= 4 || t < thresholds1_7.w) {
0006f739                 // thresholds1_7.y is mid point for intervals (0,3) and (4,7)
0006f787                 if (%d <= 2 || t < thresholds1_7.y) {
0006f7bd                     // thresholds1_7.x is mid point for intervals (0,1) and (2,3)
0006f80f                     if (%d <= 1 || t < thresholds1_7.x) {
0006f849                         %s s = scale[0]; b = bias[0];
0006f87f                     } else {
0006f89c                         %s s = scale[1]; b = bias[1];
0006f8d2                     }
0006f8e8                 } else {
0006f901                     // thresholds1_7.z is mid point for intervals (4,5) and (6,7)
0006f953                     if (%d <= 3 || t < thresholds1_7.z) {
0006f98d                         %s s = scale[2]; b = bias[2];
0006f9c3                     } else {
0006f9e0                         %s s = scale[3]; b = bias[3];
0006fa16                     }
0006fa2c                 }
0006fa3e             } else {
0006fa53                 // thresholds9_13.y is mid point for intervals (8,11) and (12,15)
0006faa5                 if (%d <= 6 || t < thresholds9_13.y) {
0006fadc                     // thresholds9_13.x is mid point for intervals (8,9) and (10,11)
0006fb31                     if (%d <= 5 || t < thresholds9_13.x) {
0006fb6c                         %s s = scale[4]; b = bias[4];
0006fba2                     } else {
0006fbbf                         %s s = scale[5]; b = bias[5];
0006fbf5                     }
0006fc0b                 } else {
0006fc24                     // thresholds9_13.z is mid point for intervals (12,13) and (14,15)
0006fc7b                     if (%d <= 7 || t < thresholds9_13.z) {
0006fcb6                         %s s = scale[6]; b = bias[6];
0006fcec                     } else {
0006fd09                         %s s = scale[7]; b = bias[7];
0006fd3f                     }
0006fd55                 }
0006fd67             }
0006fd75             return t * s + b;
0006fd93         }
0006fd9d         
0006fda7         uniform half4 thresholds[%d];
0006fdcd         uniform float4 scale[%d];
0006fdef         uniform float4 bias[%d];
0006fe11         half4 main(float2 coord) {
0006fe34             half t = half(coord.x);
0006fe59             // Choose a chunk from thresholds via binary search in a loop.
0006fea4             int low = 0;
0006febd             int high = %d;
0006fed8             int chunk = %d;
0006fef4             for (int loop = 0; loop < %d; ++loop) {
0006ff28                 if (t < thresholds[chunk].w) {
0006ff57                     high = chunk;
0006ff79                 } else {
0006ff92                     low = chunk + 1;
0006ffb7                 }
0006ffc9                 chunk = (low + high) / 2;
0006fff3             }
00070002             // Choose the final position via explicit 4-way binary search.
0007004d             int pos;
00070062             if (t < thresholds[chunk].y) {
0007008d                 pos = (t < thresholds[chunk].x) ? 0 : 1;
000700c6             } else {
000700db                 pos = (t < thresholds[chunk].z) ? 2 : 3;
00070114             }
00070122             @if (%d > 0) {
0007013d                 pos += 4 * chunk;
0007015f             }
0007016d             return t * scale[pos] + bias[pos];
0007019c         }

00070243         uniform shader colorizer;
00070265         uniform shader gradLayout;
00070289         uniform int mirror;                  // specialized
000702c5         uniform int makePremul;              // specialized
00070301         uniform int layoutPreservesOpacity;  // specialized
0007033d         uniform int useFloorAbsWorkaround;   // specialized
0007037a         half4 main(float2 coord) {
0007039d             half4 t = gradLayout.eval(coord);
000703cc             if (!bool(layoutPreservesOpacity) && t.y < 0) {
00070408                 // layout has rejected this fragment (rely on sksl to remove this branch if the
00070468                 // layout FP preserves opacity is false)
000704a1                 return half4(0);
000704c2             } else {
000704d7                 if (bool(mirror)) {
000704fb                     half t_1 = t.x - 1;
00070523                     half tiled_t = t_1 - 2 * floor(t_1 * 0.5) - 1;
00070566                     if (bool(useFloorAbsWorkaround)) {
0007059d                         // At this point the expected value of tiled_t should between -1 and 1, so
00070600                         // this clamp has no effect other than to break up the floor and abs calls
00070663                         // and make sure the compiler doesn't merge them back together.
000706bb                         tiled_t = clamp(tiled_t, -1, 1);
000706f4                     }
0007070a                     t.x = abs(tiled_t);
00070732                 } else {
0007074b                     // Simple repeat mode
00070775                     t.x = fract(t.x);
0007079b                 }
000707ae                 // Always sample from (x, 0), discarding y, since the layout FP can use y as a
0007080d                 // side-channel.
0007082e                 half4 outColor = colorizer.eval(t.x0);
00070865                 if (bool(makePremul)) {
0007088d                     outColor.rgb *= outColor.a;
000708bd                 }
000708cf                 return outColor;
000708f0             }
000708fe         }

00070974         uniform int edgeType;  // GrClipEdgeType, specialized
000709b2         // The circle uniform is (center.x, center.y, radius + 0.5, 1 / (radius + 0.5)) for regular
00070a16         // fills and (..., radius - 0.5, 1 / (radius - 0.5)) for inverse fills.
00070a66         uniform float4 circle;
00070a86         half4 main(float2 xy, half4 inColor) {
00070ab5             // TODO: Right now the distance to circle calculation is performed in a space normalized
00070b1a             // to the radius and then denormalized. This is to mitigate overflow on devices that
00070b7b             // don't have full float.
00070ba1             half d;
00070bb5             if (edgeType == kInverseFillBW || edgeType == kInverseFillAA) {
00070c01                 d = half((length((circle.xy - sk_FragCoord.xy) * circle.w) - 1.0) * circle.z);
00070c60             } else {
00070c75                 d = half((1.0 - length((circle.xy - sk_FragCoord.xy) *  circle.w)) * circle.z);
00070cd5             }

00070d28                 return inColor * saturate(d);

000710f8         uniform shader maskFP;

0007113c         uniform half outerThreshold;

0007118f             half4 mask_color = maskFP.eval(xy);
000711bf             if (mask_color.a < 0.5) {
000711e5                 if (color.a > outerThreshold) {
00071215                     half scale = outerThreshold / color.a;
00071250                     color.rgb *= scale;
00071278                     color.a = outerThreshold;
000712a6                 }
000712b8             } else if (color.a < innerThreshold) {
000712eb                 half scale = innerThreshold / max(0.001, color.a);
0007132e                 color.rgb *= scale;
00071352                 color.a = innerThreshold;
0007137c             }

000713a4         }

000713b4         uniform half4x4 m;
000713cf         uniform half4   v;
000713ea         uniform int unpremulInput;   // always specialized
00071425         uniform int clampRGBOutput;  // always specialized
00071460         uniform int premulOutput;    // always specialized
0007149b         half4 main(half4 color) {
000714bd             if (bool(unpremulInput)) {
000714e4                 color = unpremul(color);
0007150d             }
0007151b             color = m * color + v;
0007153e             if (bool(clampRGBOutput)) {
00071566                 color = saturate(color);
0007158f             } else {
000715a4                 color.a = saturate(color.a);
000715d1             }
000715df             if (bool(premulOutput)) {
00071605                 color.rgb *= color.a;
0007162b             }
00071639             return color;
00071653         }
0007165d     
00071663         half4 main(half4 color) {
00071685             half3   hsl = color.rgb;
000716ab             half      C = (1 - abs(2 * hsl.z - 1)) * hsl.y;
000716e7             half3     p = hsl.xxx + half3(0, 2/3.0, 1/3.0);
00071723             half3     q = saturate(abs(fract(p) * 6 - 3) - 1);
00071762             half3   rgb = (q - 0.5) * C + hsl.z;
00071794             color = saturate(half4(rgb, color.a));
000717c7             color.rgb *= color.a;
000717e9             return color;
00071803         }

00071839             float4 color = float4(halfColor);

0007189b             color.rgb = color.a <= 0 ? half3(0) : floor(color.rgb / color.a * 255 + 0.5) / 255;
000718fb             return color;
00071915         }

00071c25         uniform shader colorizer;
00071c47         uniform shader gradLayout;
00071c6b         uniform half4 leftBorderColor;  // t < 0.0
00071c9e         uniform half4 rightBorderColor; // t > 1.0
00071cd2         uniform int makePremul;              // specialized
00071d0e         uniform int layoutPreservesOpacity;  // specialized
00071d4b         half4 main(float2 coord) {
00071d6e             half4 t = gradLayout.eval(coord);
00071d9c             half4 outColor;
00071db9             // If t.x is below 0, use the left border color without invoking the child processor.
00071e1b             // If any t.x is above 1, use the right border color. Otherwise, t is in the [0, 1]
00071e7b             // range assumed by the colorizer FP, so delegate to the child processor.
00071ed1             if (!bool(layoutPreservesOpacity) && t.y < 0) {
00071f0d                 // layout has rejected this fragment (rely on sksl to remove this branch if the
00071f6d                 // layout FP preserves opacity is false)

00071fcb             } else if (t.x < 0) {
00071fed                 outColor = leftBorderColor;
00072019             } else if (t.x > 1.0) {
0007203d                 outColor = rightBorderColor;
0007206a             } else {

000720de                 // side-channel.
000720ff                 outColor = colorizer.eval(t.x0);
00072130             }

00072162                 outColor.rgb *= outColor.a;
